Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mac_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: acc += a*b
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
- output ports: out[37]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`a`, `b`) at one clock edge and `out` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `out` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `out` is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and `reset` is its only control (no enable, no load).
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by `reset`. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, spmv.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019

  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified spmv under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.95433 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 32 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 5.85603e+07 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 4.07248e+08. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in fetcher: no multiply, a register through six LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 5.96 ns in all, 1.60 of it into the third LUT; 7.089 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in a module not established, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with enable; wire 5.51 ns in all, 6.376 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it

spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.

  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.

Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (
        .clk   (clk),
        .reset (rst),
        .a     (acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[(2*`MAT_VAL_BITS)-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'b0),
        .b     ({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
        .out   (mac_out)
    );

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            sum_base       <= 0;
            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            row_change_reg <= row_change;
            if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Accumulator started a new row by clearing wr_data at the row-change edge and accumulating that row's first product on the following edge (instead of loading it immediately), the block's reset could be driven straight from the row change and the 32 snapshot registers plus 16-bit subtractors would not be needed at all.

2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
9	// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
10	// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
11	// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
12	// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
13	// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
14	// in output vector.
15	// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
16	// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is spmv.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (
        .clk   (clk),
        .reset (rst),
        .a     (acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[(2*`MAT_VAL_BITS)-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'b0),
        .b     ({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
        .out   (mac_out)
    );

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            sum_base       <= 0;
            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            row_change_reg <= row_change;
            if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Accumulator started a new row by clearing wr_data at the row-change edge and accumulating that row's first product on the following edge (instead of loading it immediately), the block's reset could be driven straight from the row change and the 32 snapshot registers plus 16-bit subtractors would not be needed at all.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

APPROVE

Checks performed against spmv.v:

  • Widths/lines. MULT_BITS=16, MAT_VAL_BITS=VEC_VAL_BITS=8 (spmv.v:25-29), so {vec_val,mat_val} is exactly the 16-bit mult payload and vec_val*mat_val (max 65025) also fit exactly — no truncation either way. mult_out[15:8]/[7:0] zero-extended to 18/19 is right for the unsigned contract.
  • Visibility. mult_out is local to Channel_Accumulator (spmv.v:3222), driven by fifo_mult.dout (3302) and read only at 3423/3428; Channel is instantiated once (3225). wr_data/wr_data_delay (3358,3362) are read only by fifo_data_out.din (3376) and each other; the top-level wr_data (133,175,212) is a distinct signal. Re-encoding the FIFO payload and folding both regs into the block hides nothing read elsewhere.
  • Gating. acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg matches the branch priority at 3416/3420 exactly (the ~first_read branch pre-empts accumulation and leaves wr_data held), and row_change matches 3421.
  • Recurrence. With T(j+1)=T(j)+Q_j (block) and sum_base(j+1)=row_change_reg(j)?T(j):sum_base(j), T(k)-sum_base(k)=wr_data(k-1)=wr_data_delay(k) holds at every edge that takes the start branch, including back-to-back row changes, the hold cases, and the spurious initial-row 0 write. fifo_data_out samples din with we=wr_en, so the written word is identical.
  • Reset. rst clears the block's out and operand registers (sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v:30-33), matching wr_data<=0 (3404) and discarding the in-flight term; sum_base/row_change_reg clear on the same edge. The only window where din differs (baseline's unreset wr_data_delay holding stale data after reset and through the start==0 gap) has wr_en==0 throughout, and wr_en is only ever set inside the start branch, by which point the invariant is re-established at the first start edge. Mid-run reset pulses (testbench reset_pattern) are covered.
  • ASSUMES. The "start never falls except under rst" claim is correct: spmv.v:143-154 only ever assigns start<=1 outside the reset branch. No assumption is needed, consistent with the plan.
  • X-safety of the ungated b. fifo_mult's dpram output is registered and its array is uninitialized in RTL, but the testbench's $init_zero (tb_equiv_spmv.v:52) zeroes every soft register and memory word, so mult_out is never X and a=0 never multiplies an X.
  • Left candidates. spmv.v:163 is counter_delay*16, spmv.v:172 is counter_delay*8, bvb spmv.v:2490 is counter*8 — all shift amounts consumed in-cycle, no DSP in the baseline; the stated reason holds.
  • Required items. Blocks (32), tiles (16), longest block series (1, 2.219 ns vs the slowest stage), soft logic leaving each stage with "not the slowest", and register stages before/after (5→5, consistent with the registered dpram read at 3709-3716) are all stated. The two different figures quoted for the untouched fetcher path (6.95433 vs 7.089 ns) are a cost estimate, which measurement decides.

1	//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
2	// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
7	// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
8	// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
9	// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
10	// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
11	// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
12	// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
13	// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
14	// in output vector.

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh spmv.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (
        .clk   (clk),
        .reset (rst),
        .a     (acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[(2*`MAT_VAL_BITS)-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'b0),
        .b     ({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
        .out   (mac_out)
    );

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            sum_base       <= 0;
            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            row_change_reg <= row_change;
            if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
// output width; the product alone can fill it).
// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
module mac_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [17:0] a,
    input [18:0] b,
    output reg [36:0] out
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] a_reg;
    reg [18:0] b_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            out <= 0;
            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
        end
    end

endmodule
===

module mac_int_18x19(clk, reset, a, b, out);
  input clk;
  input reset;
  input [17:0] a;
  input [18:0] b;
  output [36:0] out;
endmodule

module Channel_Accumulator(

	output vec_val_rd_en,

	output row_id_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out,
	output data_out_empty,
	output [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out,
	input out_rd_en,

	output done
	);
 
	wire mult_rd_en;
	wire mult_empty;
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out;
	wire mult_done;

module Channel(
	input clk, 
	input rst,
	input start,
	input fetcher_done,

	input [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mat_val,
	input mat_val_empty,
	output mat_val_rd_en,

	input [`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vec_val,
	input vec_val_empty,
	output vec_val_rd_en,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult_out,
	output mult_empty,
	input mult_rd_en,

	output done 
	);

	parameter FIFO_DEPTH = 8;
	// parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = `LOG2(FIFO_DEPTH);
	parameter FIFO_DEPTH_BITS = $clog2(8);

	reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] mult;
	reg mult_wr_en;
	wire mult_full;
	
	reg vec_val_rd_en_reg;
	reg mat_val_rd_en_reg;

	generic_fifo_sc_a #(
		.dw(`MULT_BITS),
		.aw(FIFO_DEPTH_BITS)
		) fifo_mult (
		.clk(clk),
		.rst(rst),
		.clr(1'b0),
		.din(mult),
		.we(mult_wr_en),
		.re(mult_rd_en),
		.dout(mult_out),
		.full(mult_full),
		.empty(mult_empty)
	);

	assign done = mat_val_empty & fetcher_done & start;

	assign vec_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);
	assign mat_val_rd_en = start & (~vec_val_empty) & (~mat_val_empty) & (~mult_full);

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		vec_val_rd_en_reg <= vec_val_rd_en;
		mat_val_rd_en_reg <= mat_val_rd_en;

		if(vec_val_rd_en_reg && mat_val_rd_en_reg) begin
			mult <= vec_val * mat_val;
			mult_wr_en <= 1;
		end
		else begin
			mult_wr_en <= 0;
		end
	end
endmodule

module generic_fifo_sc_a
	#(parameter dw=8,
	  parameter aw=8)
	(clk, rst, clr, din, we, dout, re,
	full, empty);

localparam max_size = 1<<aw;

input 			clk, rst, clr;
input 	[dw-1:0]	din;
input 			we;
output	[dw-1:0]	dout;
input 	wire		re;
output			full;
output			empty;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Local Wires
//
wire 	[dw-1:0]	din_nc;
wire 	[dw-1:0]	out_nc;
reg		[aw-1:0]	wp;
wire	[aw-1:0]	wp_pl1;
reg		[aw-1:0]	rp;
wire	[aw-1:0]	rp_pl1;
reg					gb;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Memory Block
//
dpram #(
	.AWIDTH(aw),
	.DWIDTH(dw)
	) u0 (
	.clk(clk),
	.address_a(rp),
	.wren_a(1'b0),
	.data_a(din_nc),
	.out_a(dout),
	.address_b(wp),
	.wren_b(we),
	.data_b(din),
	.out_b(out_nc)
);


////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Misc Logic
//

always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)	wp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(clr)		wp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(we)		wp <= wp_pl1;

assign wp_pl1 = wp + 1'b1;

always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)		rp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(clr)		rp <= {aw{1'b0}};
	else
	if(re)		rp <= rp_pl1;

assign rp_pl1 = rp + 1'b1;

////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
//
// Combinatorial Full & Empty Flags
//

assign empty = ((wp == rp) & !gb);
assign full  = ((wp == rp) &  gb);

// Guard Bit ...
always @(posedge clk)
	if(rst)						gb <= 1'b0;
	else
	if(clr)						gb <= 1'b0;
	else
	if((wp_pl1 == rp) & we)		gb <= 1'b1;
	else
	if(re)						gb <= 1'b0;

endmodule

module dpram #(
	parameter DWIDTH = 32,
	parameter AWIDTH = 10
  )
  (
	clk,
	address_a,
	address_b,
	wren_a,
	wren_b,
	data_a,
	data_b,
	out_a,
	out_b
);

localparam NUM_WORDS=1<<AWIDTH;

input clk;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_a;
input [(AWIDTH-1):0] address_b;
input  wren_a;
input  wren_b;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_a;
input [(DWIDTH-1):0] data_b;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_a;
output reg [(DWIDTH-1):0] out_b;

`ifndef hard_mem

	reg [DWIDTH-1:0] ram[NUM_WORDS-1:0];

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if (wren_a) begin
			ram[address_a] <= data_a;
		end
		else begin
			out_a <= ram[address_a];
		end
	end
	  
	always @ (posedge clk) begin 
		if (wren_b) begin
			ram[address_b] <= data_b;
		end 
		else begin
			out_b <= ram[address_b];
		end
	end

`else
defparam u_dual_port_ram.ADDR_WIDTH = AWIDTH;
defparam u_dual_port_ram.DATA_WIDTH = DWIDTH;

	dual_port_ram u_dual_port_ram(
	.addr1(address_a),
	.we1(wren_a),
	.data1(data_a),
	.out1(out_a),
	.addr2(address_b),
	.we2(wren_b),
	.data2(data_b),
	.out2(out_b),
	.clk(clk)
	);

`endif

endmodule

vec_val_empty = (r32 % 4) == 0;\n        draw; row_id_empty  = (r32 % 4) == 0;\n        draw; out_rd_en     = (r32 % 3) == 0;\n        draw; fetcher_done  = (r32 % 16) == 0;\n        draw; start_trig    = (r32 % 8) != 0;\n    end endtask\n\n    initial begin\n        prng = 32'h2545f491; mism = 0; cycle = 0;\n        rst = 1; start_trig = 0; fetcher_done = 0;\n        mat_val = 0; vec_val = 0; row_id = 0;\n        mat_val_empty = 1; vec_val_empty = 1; row_id_empty = 1; out_rd_en = 0;\n        repeat (4) @(negedge clk);\n        rst = 0;\n        for (cycle = 0; cycle < 40000; cycle = cycle + 1) begin\n            @(negedge clk);\n            if (data_out_r !== data_out_d || addr_out_r !== addr_out_d ||\n                de_r !== de_d || done_r !== done_d ||\n                mve_r !== mve_d || vve_r !== vve_d || rie_r !== rie_d) begin\n                mism = mism + 1;\n                if (mism < 8) $display(\"MISMATCH cycle=%0d t=%0t data %h/%h addr %h/%h empty %b/%b done %b/%b rden %b%b%b/%b%b%b\",\n                    cycle, $time, data_out_r, data_out_d, addr_out_r, addr_out_d, de_r, de_d, done_r, done_d,\n                    mve_r,vve_r,rie_r, mve_d,vve_d,rie_d);\n            end\n            set_random;\n            draw; rst = (r32 % 64) == 0;\n        end\n        $display(\"self-check: %0d mismatches over %0d cycles\", mism, cycle);\n        $finish;\n    end\nendmodule\n"
