prompt
Task

Edit spmv.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Region in the approved plan: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (module Channel, operation multiply, datatype int8). The framework found no sum that its products feed.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh spmv.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (
        .clk   (clk),
        .reset (rst),
        .a     (acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[(2*`MAT_VAL_BITS)-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'b0),
        .b     ({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
        .out   (mac_out)
    );

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            sum_base       <= 0;
            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            row_change_reg <= row_change;
            if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

Fix to the previous attempt

The previous edit was functionally right but diverged on X. The vector buffer is 128 words behind an 8-bit address (spmv.v:37,58), so vec_val is unknown for out-of-range column ids. In the original, one unknown operand makes the 16-bit product and the row sum unknown only until the next row change reloads wr_data; the block's accumulator cannot forget an unknown, so out stayed X permanently and 541 extra X cycles reached dout.

I added, in Accumulator, the part the plan's mapping was missing — the X path, as logic that only reproduces the original's behavior:

  • mult_x = (^mult_out === 1'bx) detects an unknown term; a and b are forced to 0 for such a term, keeping the block's accumulator defined. Dropping that term is invisible: the row it belongs to is reported unknown anyway, and sum_base is re-snapshotted at the next row change, so no later row is affected.
  • wr_data_x / wr_data_x_delay, one-bit shadows of wr_data / wr_data_delay, carry the unknown with exactly the baseline's load/accumulate/hold/reset structure, and row_sum returns all-X whenever wr_data_x_delay is set.
  • row_change now uses ((row_id != wr_addr) === 1'b1), matching how if(row_id!=wr_addr) resolves an unknown comparison.

This folds to nothing under techmap (there is no unknown in synthesis). Measured hierarchically with the memory pass run: pristine 36017 cells, rewrite without the X path 24849, rewrite as delivered 25169 — the X tracking costs 320 cells (10 per channel, ~1.3%) against the ~11000 the rewrite removes; memories are unchanged (229 $mem_v2) in all three. No ASSUMES: is needed.

module spmv(
	input clk,
	input rst,

	output [`MULT_BITS-1:0] dout,

	output reg done_reg,

  input [`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0] mat_val_din,
  input	[`COL_ID_BITS-1:0] col_id_din,
  input [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] row_id_din,

  input mat_val_wren,
  input col_id_wren,
  input row_id_wren,

  input [`MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] mat_val_addr_ext,
  input [`COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] col_id_addr_ext,
  input [`ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH-1:0] row_id_addr_ext,

	input [`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR*`VEC_VAL_BITS-1:0] vector_din,
  input vector_wren,
  input [`BVB_AWIDTH-1:0] vector_addr_ext
);

	// Row ID fifo signal
	wire [(`ROW_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] row_id;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] row_id_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] row_id_rd_en;

	// column id fifo signals
	wire [(`COL_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL-1):0] col_id;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] col_id_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] col_id_rd_en; 

	// matrix elements fifo signals
	wire [(`MAT_VAL_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] mat_val;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] mat_val_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] mat_val_rd_en;

	// The above the FIFOs are called fetcher FIFOs as they are a part of fetcher module and are filled by fetcher.

	// Fetcher module signals. There are just the above three put together.
	wire [((`ROW_ID_BITS+`COL_ID_BITS+`MAT_VAL_BITS)*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] fetcher_out;
	wire [3*`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] fetcher_empty;
	wire [3*`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] fetcher_rd_en;

	// Vector values fifo signals
	wire [`VEC_VAL_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] vec_val;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] vec_val_empty;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] vec_val_rd_en;

	// 1 if at least 1 of the fetcher FIFOs is empty
	reg all_empty;

	// Signal to start the engine
	reg start;

	// Output data
	wire [(`MULT_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] data_out;
	// output fifo empty signals
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] data_out_empty;
	// Address to store output data
	wire [(`ROW_ID_BITS*`NUM_CHANNEL)-1:0] addr_out;
	// output fifo read enable signal
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en;
	// reg [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en_reg;
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] out_rd_en_shifted;
	// Memory to write the output data

	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] data_out_shifted;
	wire [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] addr_out_shifted;
	wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] wr_data;
	wire [`ROW_ID_BITS-1:0] wr_addr;
	reg wr_en;

	// Signals to indicate that the computation is complete
	wire [`NUM_CHANNEL-1:0] done;
	reg done_all, last;

	wire fetcher_done;

	always@(posedge clk) begin
		if (rst) begin
			all_empty <= 1;
			start <= 0;
		end
		else begin
			all_empty <= (|fetcher_empty) & (~start);
			if (!all_empty) begin
				start <= 1;
			end
		end
	end

	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter;
	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter_delay;
	reg [`NUM_CHANNEL_BITS-1:0] counter_store;

	assign out_rd_en = (({`NUM_CHANNEL{1'b0}})|(1<<counter)) & ~data_out_empty;
	assign out_rd_en_shifted = out_rd_en >> counter;

	assign data_out_shifted = data_out >> (counter_delay*`MULT_BITS);
`ifdef QUARTUS
	genvar loop_idx;
	generate
		for (loop_idx = 0; loop_idx < `ROW_ID_BITS; loop_idx = loop_idx + 1) begin: gen_addr_out
			assign addr_out_shifted[loop_idx] = ^(addr_out[(loop_idx+1)*`NUM_CHANNEL-1:loop_idx*`NUM_CHANNEL]);
		end
	endgenerate
`else
	assign addr_out_shifted = addr_out >> (counter_delay*`ROW_ID_BITS);
`endif

	assign wr_data = data_out_shifted;
	assign wr_addr = addr_out_shifted;

	always @ (posedge clk) begin
		if(rst) begin
			counter <= 0;
			counter_delay <= 0;
			last <= 0;
		end
		else if(start) begin
			done_all <= &done;
			counter <= counter + 1;
			counter_delay <= counter;
			if(out_rd_en_shifted[0]) begin
				wr_en <= 1;
			end
			else begin
				wr_en <= 0;
			end
			if(done_all & !last) begin
				counter_store <= counter_delay;
				last <= 1;
			end
			else if (last) begin
				done_reg <= (counter_store==counter_delay);
			end
		end
	end

	spram #(
		.AWIDTH(`ROW_ID_BITS),
		.NUM_WORDS(`NUM_VEC_VALS),
		.DWIDTH(`MULT_BITS)
	) write_mem (
		.clk(clk),
		.address(wr_addr),
		.wren(wr_en),
		.din(wr_data),
		.dout(dout)
	);

	// Assign FIFOs' read enables to th
…[truncated, 6553 chars total]

 "content": "/* A stand-in for the harness's $init_zero VPI, for local reproduction only.\n   $init_zero(model_name_string, inst1, inst2, ...) sets every reg / integer /\n   memory word under each instance to 0, skipping scopes whose definition name\n   is the hard-block model (its registers are unknown at power-up). */\n#include <vpi_user.h>\n#include <string.h>\n#include <stdlib.h>\n\nstatic char model[256] = \"\";\n\nstatic void zero_handle(vpiHandle h)\n{\n    s_vpi_value v;\n    v.format = vpiIntVal;\n    v.value.integer = 0;\n    vpi_put_value(h, &v, NULL, vpiNoDelay);\n}\n\nstatic void zero_scope(vpiHandle scope)\n{\n    vpiHandle it, h;\n    const char *dn = vpi_get_str(vpiDefName, scope);\n    int skip = (dn && model[0] && strcmp(dn, model) == 0);\n\n    if (!skip) {\n        it = vpi_iterate(vpiReg, scope);\n        if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) zero_handle(h);\n        it = vpi_iterate(vpiIntegerVar, scope);\n        if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) zero_handle(h);\n        it = vpi_iterate(vpiMemory, scope);\n        if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) {\n            vpiHandle it2 = vpi_iterate(vpiMemoryWord, h), w;\n            if (it2) while ((w = vpi_scan(it2))) zero_handle(w);\n        }\n    }\n\n    it = vpi_iterate(vpiInternalScope, scope);\n    if (it) while ((h = vpi_scan(it))) zero_scope(h);\n}\n\nstatic PLI_INT32 init_zero_calltf(PLI_BYTE8 *ud)\n{\n    vpiHandle systf = vpi_handle(vpiSysTfCall, NULL);\n    vpiHandle it = vpi_iterate(vpiArgument, systf), a;\n    int n = 0;\n    (void)ud;\n    if (!it) return 0;\n    while ((a = vpi_scan(it))) {\n        n++;\n        if (n == 1) {\n            s_vpi_value v; v.format = vpiStringVal;\n            vpi_get_value(a, &v);\n            if (v.value.str) { strncpy(model, v.value.str, sizeof(model) - 1); }\n        } else {\n            zero_scope(a);\n        }\n    }\n    vpi_printf(\"init_zero: model \\\"%s\\\", %d instance(s) zeroed\\n\", model, n - 1);\n    return 0;\n}\n\nstatic PLI_INT32 init_zero_compiletf(PLI_BYTE8 *ud) { (void)ud; return 0; }\n\nstatic void register_tf(void)\n{\n    s_vpi_systf_data d;\n    d.type = vpiSysTask;\n    d.sysfunctype = 0;\n    d.tfname = \"$init_zero\";\n    d.calltf = init_zero_calltf;\n    d.compiletf = init_zero_compiletf;\n    d.sizetf = NULL;\n    d.user_data = NULL;\n    vpi_register_systf(&d);\n}\n\nvoid (*vlog_startup_routines[])(void) = { register_tf, 0 };\n"

output
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Matrix vector multiplication design with sparsity
// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of 
// the operation. The input to the accelerator are 3 values corresponding 
// to each matrix element: data, col, number of non zero values in a row. 
// There are 32 channels which can run in parallel. Each channel has a MAC 
// unit. Computation for each row of the matrix is assigned to a channel 
// dynamically based on the number of non zero values in that row. The final
// accumulated value is written in the output memory. The write address is 
// based on row number. Hence, write is out of order with respect to rows 
// in output vector.
// Multipliers in channels followed by accumulators, Banked Vector Buffer 
// (RAM and crossbar) to store vector elements. Arbiter to fetch data from 
// RAMs store matrix elements. Uses CISR encoding. Multiple FIFOs.
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////
// Author: Aatman Borda
//////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////

//`define SIMULATION
`define COL_ID_BITS 8
`define ROW_ID_BITS 8
`define MAT_VAL_BITS 8
`define VEC_VAL_BITS 8
`define MULT_BITS (`VEC_VAL_BITS + `MAT_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_CHANNEL 32
`define NUM_CHANNEL_BITS $clog2(`NUM_CHANNEL)
`define LANE_NUM (3 * `NUM_CHANNEL)
// `define LANE_NUM_BITS $clog2(`LANE_NUM)
//`define NUM_MAT_VALS 8864
`define NUM_MAT_VALS 4096
`define NUM_COL_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_ROW_IDS `NUM_MAT_VALS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS 128

//`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH $clog2(`NUM_MAT_VALS)
`define MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH 12
`define COL_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH
`define ROW_ID_ADDR_WIDTH `MAT_VAL_ADDR_WIDTH

`define FIFO_DEPTH 8
`define MAX_COLS (1<<`COL_ID_BITS)

// MACROS for BVB
`define BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM 1
`define NUM_BRAMS 1
`define VEC_VAL_BYTES (`VEC_VAL_BITS/8)
`define VEC_VAL_OFFSET $clog2(`VEC_VAL_BITS)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM (`BYTES_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM/`VEC_VAL_BYTES)
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_PER_BRAM*`NUM_BRAMS
`define NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS $clog2(`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)
`define NUM_ADDR (`NUM_VEC_VALS/`NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR)+1
`define BVB_AWIDTH `COL_ID_BITS
`define COUNTER_BITS $clog2(`NUM_ADDR)
`define LOCAL_ID_BITS `NUM_VEC_VALS_PER_ADDR_BITS

3364	
3365		// The per-channel product and its row accumulation live in the hard block.
3366		// mult_out now carries the operand pair {vec_val, mat_val} from fifo_mult.
3367		wire [36:0] mac_out;
3368		wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
3369		wire row_change = acc_en & ((row_id != wr_addr) === 1'b1);
3370		reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
3371		reg row_change_reg;
3372	
3373		// An unknown operand makes the baseline's 16-bit product unknown, and with it
3374		// the whole row sum, until the next row change reloads wr_data from a fresh
3375		// product. The block's accumulator cannot forget an unknown, so such a term is
3376		// kept off its ports (its contribution is dropped, which no output can see: the

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file spmv.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog

    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (

        .reset (rst),

            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Accumulator started a new row by clearing wr_data at the row-change edge and accumulating that row's first product on the following edge (instead of loading it immediately), the block's reset could be driven straight from the row change and the 32 snapshot registers plus 16-bit subtractors would not be needed at all.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan conformance

  • Channel (spmv.v:3317): only the one line changed, mult <= {vec_val, mat_val}. mult is MULT_BITS=16 (VEC_VAL_BITS+MAT_VAL_BITS = 8+8, spmv.v:26-29), so the pair fits exactly; mult_wr_en, the two _rd_en_regs and the condition are byte-for-byte unchanged, so FIFO timing/flags downstream are untouched.
  • Accumulator (spmv.v:3358-3483): wr_data/wr_data_delay and their four assignments are gone; one mac_int_18x19 added; fifo_data_out.din is row_sum; fifo_addr_out.din still wr_addr_delay; wr_en/wr_addr/wr_addr_delay/first_read/last/done and the branch structure are as the plan requires. No module mac_int_18x19 added — instantiated by name only (spmv.v:3385, the sole instantiation, elaborating to 32 with Channel_Accumulator).
  • Deviations from the plan text, both additive and both inside the region the plan edits: (row_id != wr_addr) === 1'b1 in row_change, and the mult_x/term_en/wr_data_x/wr_data_x_delay X shadows. Both are behaviour-reproducing only: ===1'b1 matches Verilog if semantics (baseline takes the row-change branch only when the compare is 1), and the X shadows are identically 0 in any X-free run, so the synthesizable design is exactly the plan's. Allowed under "logic added only to reproduce it".

Ports — all four inputs driven: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a 18 bits ({10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} = vec_val, gated), .b 19 bits ({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]} = mat_val). out drives only row_sum → fifo_data_out.din, as planned.

Timing/value equivalence — verified the invariant T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1) against the model in sim_reference/mac_int_18x19.v: T(k+1)=T(k)+Q_k, sum_base(j)=R_{j-1}?T(j-1):sum_base(j-1); induction holds for the load (R=1 → Q_j), accumulate, and hold cases, and mac_out[15:0]-sum_base reproduces the 16-bit wrap because every operation is modular addition and an 8×8 product fits in 16 bits. acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg reproduces the baseline's branch guard exactly.

Reset — rst drives .reset directly; the block clears both its operand register and out at the same edge the baseline clears wr_data, and the pending operand is discarded exactly as the baseline's if(rst) discards that cycle's term, so the invariant re-establishes at the first post-reset edge. The one cycle where din differs (the reset edge, baseline wr_data_delay holds stale data) is a cycle with wr_en=0, and wr_en can only be re-raised from inside the start branch after first_read — confirmed start is set only to 1 and cleared only by rst (spmv.v:143-154). Added registers: sum_base/row_change_reg are rst-cleared; wr_data_x_delay is not, but it is written in every start cycle before wr_en can first be 1, so no reliance on its initial value.

Visibility — mult_out is local to Channel_Accumulator (spmv.v:3222), read only by A0 (3388-3389, 3466, 3471); Channel has one instantiation, so redefining the FIFO payload as the operand pair is contained. The top-level wr_data (spmv.v:133/175/212) is a different module's wire and is untouched. b is gated by mult_x as well as a — necessary, since 0 * X is X in the model and would poison the accumulator permanently.

Terms/scope — the single product term is preserved; the remaining * at spmv.v:163, 172, 2490 are untouched shift amounts. Only whitespace otherwise (end → end at 3451).

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

prompt

5 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, spmv.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mac_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: acc += a*b
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], a[18], b[19]
  • output ports: out[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (a, b) at one clock edge and out at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in out after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. out is the running sum: it adds its term (one product) on every clock edge that does not clear it, and reset is its only control (no enable, no load).
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers and the running sum) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the running sum stays unknown until a clear by reset. The block must be cleared before its first result is used.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of out (clock-to-Q)0.060
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages.
  • A DSP tile holds two blocks of this mode. Blocks in one tile share one clock and one reset, so blocks on different reset signals cannot share a tile.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified spmv under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 6.95433 ns on its one clock clk (I/O pads not timed) and uses 32 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 5.85603e+07 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 4.07248e+08. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in fetcher: no multiply, a register through six LUTs into a flip-flop with synchronous reset and enable; wire 5.96 ns in all, 1.60 of it into the third LUT; 7.089 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in a module not established, is no multiply, a register through five LUTs into a flip-flop with enable; wire 5.51 ns in all, 6.376 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: multiply.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products reach no sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: no sum.

idinstanceswhat it already is in the baseline
Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$7532on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, one to a tile on all 32 tiles (96 of the 128 slots open)
bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$571no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$111no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$131no DSP tile; a product by a constant factor that synthesis turns into shifts, so the baseline netlist holds no multiply cell for it
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
TILES: <how many DSP tiles the whole design then uses, one integer>
STAGE_NS: <the delay of the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, in ns, one number>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. spmv.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75
SHARED_REDUCTION: Accumulator.wr_data (the per-channel row sum; one block per channel, 32 in all)
BLOCKS: 32
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.95433
MAPPING: per Channel_Accumulator instance, one mac_int_18x19: .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a(acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[15:8]} : 18'b0) (= vec_val, zero-extended, gated by the accumulate condition), .b({11'b0, mult_out[7:0]}) (= mat_val, zero-extended, ungated), .out(mac_out[36:0]); only mac_out[15:0] is used, as row_sum = mac_out[15:0] - sum_base, which becomes fifo_data_out.din.

PLAN:

What is converted, and why this shape

The one product worth moving is the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317, 32 instances). It cannot be converted in place: in the baseline its operands sit in a register (the FIFO's dpram output register) and its result is captured one edge later in mult, i.e. one register stage, while the block needs two (operands before edge k → out after edge k+1). Any in-place swap delays the write into fifo_mult by a cycle, which delays mult_empty, the Accumulator handshake, data_out, and therefore the round-robin write to write_mem — the testbench compares dout and done_reg on every cycle, so that fails.

So the product is regrouped (T2) across the fifo_mult boundary and fused with the accumulation it feeds:

  1. Channel keeps its register but stops multiplying. fifo_mult is 16 bits wide and vec_val,mat_val are 8+8, so the FIFO can carry the operand pair instead of the product. Identical width, identical we/re/flags, identical timing — nothing downstream of the FIFO's control moves.
  2. The Accumulator multiplies and accumulates in the block. Accumulator already sums those products into wr_data and then delays it one cycle into wr_data_delay before the output FIFO. That spare delay register is exactly the extra stage the block needs: the block's out is wr_data delayed by one cycle, i.e. it is wr_data_delay, with the right value in the right cycle. Both wr_data (16 FF + 16-bit adder + load/hold mux) and wr_data_delay (16 FF) disappear into the block.

This frees all 32 mult_9x9_fixed_pt tiles and adds 32 blocks, 2 to a tile (every block is on clk/rst, so they may share tiles): 32 tiles → 16.

Candidates left, with the reason: bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 are all a free-running counter times a power of two (counter*8, counter_delay*16, counter_delay*8) used as a shift amount in the same cycle. They are wires after synthesis (no multiply cell, no tile), so putting them on a block only adds tiles and area, and the block's 2-cycle latency cannot be hidden: the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value. Left as they are.

Edits
A. module Channel, the always block at 3312–3323

Replace only line 3317:

verilog
            mult <= {vec_val, mat_val};   // operand pair; the product is formed in Accumulator's block

Everything else in that block (the two _rd_en_reg registers, the condition, mult_wr_en) stays byte for byte. mult stays [MULT_BITS-1:0] = 16 bits; {vec_val, mat_val} is exactly 16 bits, so nothing is lost (the 8×8 product also fitted exactly in 16, so neither form truncates). mult when mult_wr_en is 0 remains a don't-care, as in the baseline — fifo_mult only samples din when we` is high.

B. module Accumulator

Delete the registers wr_data (3358) and wr_data_delay (3362) and their four assignments: wr_data<=0; (3404), wr_data_delay <= wr_data; (3414), wr_data <= mult_out; (3423) and wr_data <= wr_data + mult_out; (3428). Leave the branch structure, wr_en, wr_addr, wr_addr_delay, first_read, last and done exactly as they are — the row-change branch just keeps wr_en <= 1; wr_addr <= row_id;.

Add:

verilog
    wire [36:0] mac_out;
    wire acc_en     = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
    wire row_change = acc_en & (row_id != wr_addr);
    reg  [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
    reg  row_change_reg;

    mac_int_18x19 mac0 (
        .clk   (clk),
        .reset (rst),
        .a     (acc_en ? {10'b0, mult_out[(2*`MAT_VAL_BITS)-1:`MAT_VAL_BITS]} : 18'b0),
        .b     ({11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
        .out   (mac_out)
    );

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (rst) begin
            sum_base       <= 0;
            row_change_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            row_change_reg <= row_change;
            if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
        end
    end

    wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base;

and change fifo_data_out's .din(wr_data_delay) (3376) to .din(row_sum). fifo_addr_out keeps .din(wr_addr_delay).

Do not add a definition of mac_int_18x19 to spmv.v; instantiate the primitive by name only (the flow supplies the model / black box).

C. Why this is cycle-for-cycle the same

Write Q_j for the term the baseline adds at edge j (0 when it does not accumulate) and R_j for a row change at edge j. Baseline: wr_data(j) = (R_j ? 0 : wr_data(j-1)) + Q_j, and the output FIFO's din during cycle (j, j+1) is wr_data_delay(j) = wr_data(j-1).

The block, fed with the same gating, gives T(j) = T(j-1) + Q_{j-1} (operands before edge j-1 land in out after edge j), and sum_base(j) = R_{j-1} ? T(j-1) : sum_base(j-1). By induction T(j) - sum_base(j) = wr_data(j-1):

  • R_j=0: T(j+1)-sum_base(j+1) = wr_data(j-1)+Q_j = wr_data(j).
  • R_j=1: = T(j)+Q_j-T(j) = Q_j = wr_data(j) (the load of a new row's first product).
  • hold (!start, !first_read, no cond): Q_j=0, R_j=0, value unchanged, as wr_data holds.
  • rst at edge j: rst clears the block's out and its operand registers, so T(j+1)=0, and sum_base/row_change_reg are cleared in the same edge, matching wr_data<=0. The din sampled at the rst edge is the pre-reset value in both designs, so a write in flight at that edge still writes the same data. Note wr_data_delay is not reset in the baseline, so after a reset the two din values differ — invisible, because rst clears wr_en, and wr_en can only be set again from inside the start branch, by which point the invariant holds again.
  • start never falls except under rst (spmv.v:143-154: start is only ever set to 1, and only rst clears it), and wr_en is 0 throughout the pre-start window, so the one window where the baseline's wr_data_delay holds stale data is a window with no writes. No ASSUMES: is needed; this is a property of the design, not of its stimulus.

The multiply is unsigned 8×8 in both (the operands are plain reg [7:0] wires), the product fits in 37 bits, and the running sum's low 16 bits wrap exactly as the baseline's 16-bit wr_data does, so taking mac_out[15:0] and subtracting mod 2^16 reproduces it. The block is cleared by the testbench's 4-cycle rst before any compared cycle, and by every mid-run rst pulse, so its power-up X never reaches dout: wr_en is 0 out of reset. sum_base/row_change_reg are added registers and are given no initial value — they are cleared by the same rst.

Visibility: mult_out is a wire local to Channel_Accumulator (3222) read only by Accumulator (3423, 3428) — nothing else sees the product, so re-defining fifo_mult's payload as the operand pair is contained. wr_data/wr_data_delay are local regs of Accumulator read only by each other and by fifo_data_out.din, so folding them into the block hides nothing that is read elsewhere. The top-level wr_data at spmv.v:133/175/212 is a different signal in a different module and is untouched.

Blocks, tiles, stages
  • Blocks added: 32 (one per Channel_Accumulator). Longest series of blocks in any stage: 1 — no block drives another; the block's own stage is 2.219 ns, well under the 6.95433 ns critical path, and it is not the slowest stage.
  • Tiles: 16. The baseline's 32 tiles are all the mult_9x9_fixed_pt instances of this one product; after the rewrite the Channels hold no multiply at all, so those 32 tiles go away and 32 blocks at 2 per tile take 16. All 32 blocks are on the single clk and the single global rst, so nothing prevents pairing. If the packer spreads them one to a tile (as it did with the 9x9s), the design uses 32 tiles — the same DSP area as the baseline, and the soft-logic saving below still stands, so the downside is bounded at roughly break-even.

  • Soft logic leaving a stage:

    • Out of the mult_out → wr_data stage: the 16-bit adder and its load/hold mux, and 16 FFs; out of wr_data → wr_data_delay: 16 FFs. That stage is not the slowest (the slowest is the no-multiply fetcher path, 7.089 ns on the seed-1 report), so this does not shorten the period.
    • Out of the Channel's vec_val/mat_val → mult stage: the whole multiply (it becomes a wire concatenation into the same register). Also not the slowest stage.
    • Added soft logic per channel: an 8-bit AND gate ahead of port a (one small stage: FF clk-to-Q 0.060 + one LUT + block setup 0.019, ≈0.4 ns), and a 16-bit subtract out of the block into the output FIFO's RAM write data (0.060 + a 16-bit carry chain + RAM setup, ≈1 ns) plus 17 FFs (sum_base, row_change_reg). Net soft: about 15 FFs fewer and a comparable LUT count per channel, so used-CLB area does not grow.
  • Slowest stage the rewrite leaves: 6.95433 ns, the untouched fetcher register-to-register path. No stage the rewrite creates comes near it.
  • Register stages, operands to result: before — vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → wr_data (4) → wr_data_delay (5) → output FIFO write. After — vec_val/mat_val → mult as the operand pair (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out, which is the running row sum (5) → output FIFO write. Five stages before, five after; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 instead of before stage 1, and its sum lives in the block's output register instead of in wr_data/wr_data_delay.

Expected objective: area falls by 16 tiles ≈ 4.06e6 of 5.856e7 (≈7%) with the period unchanged, so area × delay ≈ 3.79e8 against the baseline's 4.07248e8.

DESIGN_CHANGE: if Accumulator started a new row by clearing wr_data at the row-change edge and accumulating that row's first product on the following edge (instead of loading it immediately), the block's reset could be driven straight from the row change and the 32 snapshot registers plus 16-bit subtractors would not be needed at all.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_spmv.v) and the block's behavioural model (mac_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: spmv.v

response

I'll read the design source first.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
TILES: 16
STAGE_NS: 6.52544
MAPPING: NONE (no new rewrite; the kept candidate's mapping stands: per Channel_Accumulator, one mac_int_18x19 with .clk(clk), .reset(rst), .a = gated vec_val from mult_out[15:8], .b = mult_out[7:0], .out = the running row sum, read as mac_out[15:0] - sum_base)

PLAN:

I propose no further rewrite. The best result so far — the first-pass T2 conversion of Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 into 32 mac_int_18x19 blocks on 16 DSP tiles — is the best available from the candidate set I was given, and the run should end keeping it (area 3.30089e+07, period 6.52544 ns, area × delay 2.15398e+08, −47.11% against the baseline's 4.07248e+08; the margin is far outside the ~8.9% seed noise, so the median over all seeds will stay below the original).

Every candidate, converted or left

  • Channel.$mul$spmv.v:3317$75 (32 instances) — already converted by the kept candidate: the per-channel vec_val * mat_val (spmv.v:3317) is carried through fifo_mult as the operand pair and multiplied-and-accumulated in the block inside Accumulator, with wr_data (3358, 3404, 3423, 3428) and wr_data_delay (3362, 3414) folded into the block's accumulator and output register. There is no second, better shape for it: each channel accumulates its own independent row sum one product per cycle, so no two channels can share a block, and 32 blocks at two per tile (all on the single clk and single rst) is already the minimum tile count, 16. Nothing is left over to convert.
  • bvb.$mul$spmv.v:2490$57, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:163$11, spmv.$mul$spmv.v:172$13 (1 instance each) — left, one shared reason. Each is a free-running counter times a power of two used as a barrel-shift amount in the same cycle it is produced: rd_addr <= id >> (counter*COL_ID_BITS) (spmv.v:2490, counter*8), data_out_shifted = data_out >> (counter_delay*MULT_BITS) (spmv.v:163, counter_delay*16), addr_out_shifted = addr_out >> (counter_delay*ROW_ID_BITS) (spmv.v:172, counter_delay*8). Synthesis turns them into wiring (no multiply cell, no tile in the baseline), so a block for one of them is pure added cost — at best 4.5 CLBs of tile area when it shares a tile, 9 when it does not — against a region that costs zero today. The block also cannot carry them: its result appears two cycles after its operands, while the shift amount is consumed combinationally in the cycle the counter holds that value, and the shifted result feeds the round-robin write path (wr_data/wr_addr into write_mem, spmv.v:175-213) and bvb's rd_addr, both compared on every cycle by the testbench. Re-timing the counters to compensate would change out_rd_en/val_wr_en` alignment, i.e. change what the design computes. Converting any of the three raises area with no path shortened, so area × delay can only rise.

Blocks and tiles. This proposal adds 0 blocks. The design as kept instantiates 32 blocks and uses 16 DSP tiles (measured: 16 packed at seed 1), down from the baseline's 32.

Longest series of blocks in any stage. 1 — no block drives another; the block's own input-register-to-output-register stage is 2.219 ns, well under the kept design's 6.52544 ns period, so it is not the slowest stage and adds no pressure on the period.

What soft logic left which stage, and whether that stage was slowest. Already accounted for by the kept candidate and confirmed by measurement: the 8×8 multiply left the vec_val/mat_val → mult stage in Channel, and the 16-bit accumulate adder, its load/hold mux and the two 16-bit register banks (wr_data, wr_data_delay) left the mult_out → wr_data → wr_data_delay stages in Accumulator; neither was the slowest stage, which is why the period fell only 6.17% while area fell 43.63%. No soft logic leaves any stage under this proposal.

Register stages, operands to result. Unchanged from the kept candidate, and already five before and five after: vec_val/mat_val → mult (1) → fifo_mult RAM write (2) → RAM read into mult_out (3) → block input register (4) → block out (5) → output FIFO write; the product now forms between stages 4 and 5 rather than before stage 1.

Why nothing further is expected to lower area × delay. Both terms of the objective are now bounded by things outside the candidate set. Area: of the kept design's 3.30089e+07, the 16 DSP tiles account for 4.06e+06, and the only soft arithmetic the rewrite still leaves per channel is the sum_base register and the 16-bit subtract that turns the block's running sum back into the per-row sum. Removing those would mean driving the block's reset from each channel's row change, which (a) puts every block on its own reset signal, so no two blocks may share a tile — 16 tiles back, +4.06e+06, against roughly 32 × (16 FF + one 16-bit subtract) ≈ 2.8e+06 saved — and (b) is not even correct as it stands, since a synchronous clear also wipes the block's operand registers and so drops the product in flight, the very product that must become the new row's first term; restoring it costs the registers the change was meant to save. Delay: the period is now set by an untouched, routing-dominated path — a flip-flop through three LUTs into a single_port_ram address pin, 5.90 ns of its 6.52 ns being wire — with no multiply, no adder and no block anywhere on it. No regrouping of the remaining candidate arithmetic touches that path, and the cost guidance is explicit that a rewrite which does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster.

DESIGN_CHANGE: two changes would each let the block do more. First, if Accumulator started a new row by clearing its sum at the row-change edge and accumulating that row's first product on the following edge (instead of loading it immediately), and the 32 channels' row changes were allowed to be pairwise shared or the architecture gave each block its own reset, the block's reset could be driven straight from the row change and the 32 sum_base registers plus 16-bit subtractors would disappear. Second, the two output crossbars (data_out >> (counter_delay*16) at spmv.v:163 and addr_out >> (counter_delay*8) at spmv.v:172) are large soft barrel shifters over 512 and 256 bits; replacing the round-robin shift-select with a registered one-hot read port per channel would cut considerably more CLB area than any remaining arithmetic region can, but it is a change to the design, not a rewrite onto the block.

3	// This is a Sparse Matrix-Vector Multiplication accelerator design. 
4	// The mathematical formula is Y = M * X where M is input sparse matrix, 
5	// X is input dense vector and Y is output dense vector. We support 8-bit 
6	// precision. Vector X is loaded in on-chip banked memory before start of

1	// mac_int_18x19: out accumulates a*b, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the running sum wraps at the
2	// output width; the product alone can fill it).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, a[18], b[19] in; out[37] out.
4	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): a and b are registered
5	// at the block's entry at one clock edge; at the next, out <= out + a*b from those
6	// registers, so out is the accumulator. Cycles: a, b to out 2. A product, once added, stays in
7	// out until reset: reset is the accumulator's only control (no enable, no load).
8	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
9	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
10	// every register of the block (a, b registers and out) is cleared to 0; the block loads again at the
11	// next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is X until the first
12	// clear, so out is X until the block is reset.
13	module mac_int_18x19 (
14	    input clk,
15	    input reset,
16	    input [17:0] a,
17	    input [18:0] b,
18	    output reg [36:0] out
19	);
20	
21	    // stage 1: the input registers
22	    reg [17:0] a_reg;
23	    reg [18:0] b_reg;
24	
25	    // combinational middle: the product and the add to the running sum
26	    wire [36:0] prod = a_reg * b_reg;                             // 18x19 -> 37, exact
27	    wire [36:0] sum  = out + prod;                                // modulo 2^37
28	
29	    always @(posedge clk) begin
30	        if (reset) begin
31	            out <= 0;
32	            a_reg <= 0; b_reg <= 0;
33	        end
34	        else begin
35	            a_reg <= a; b_reg <= b;
36	            out <= sum;                                          // stage 2: the accumulator
37	        end
38	    end
39	
40	endmodule

+ // The per-channel product and its row accumulation live in the hard block.
+ // mult_out now carries the operand pair {vec_val, mat_val} from fifo_mult.
+ wire [36:0] mac_out;
+ wire acc_en = start & first_read & row_id_rd_en_reg & mult_rd_en_reg;
+ wire row_change = acc_en & ((row_id != wr_addr) === 1'b1);
+ reg [`MULT_BITS-1:0] sum_base;
+ reg row_change_reg;
+
+ // An unknown operand makes the baseline's 16-bit product unknown, and with it
+ // the whole row sum, until the next row change reloads wr_data from a fresh
+ // product. The block's accumulator cannot forget an unknown, so such a term is
+ // kept off its ports (its contribution is dropped, which no output can see: the
+ // row it belongs to is reported unknown anyway) and the unknown is carried in
+ // wr_data_x / wr_data_x_delay, the one-bit shadows of the baseline's wr_data /
+ // wr_data_delay. All of this folds away in synthesis, where there is no unknown.
+ wire mult_x = (^mult_out === 1'bx);
+ wire term_en = acc_en & ~mult_x;
+ reg wr_data_x;
+ reg wr_data_x_delay;

+ .clk (clk),

+ .b (mult_x ? 19'b0 : {11'b0, mult_out[`MAT_VAL_BITS-1:0]}),
+ .out (mac_out)
+ );
+
+ // mac_out is the running sum of every product since reset, delayed one cycle
+ // relative to the old wr_data; sum_base holds the running sum as it stood at
+ // the start of the current row, so the difference is the old wr_data_delay.
+ always @(posedge clk) begin
+ if (rst) begin
+ sum_base <= 0;
+ row_change_reg <= 0;
+ end
+ else begin
+ row_change_reg <= row_change;
+ if (row_change_reg) sum_base <= mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0];
+ end
+ end
+
+ wire [`MULT_BITS-1:0] row_sum = wr_data_x_delay ? {`MULT_BITS{1'bx}}
+ : (mac_out[`MULT_BITS-1:0] - sum_base);

Objective loop

1 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed

Pass 1
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 5.85603e+07 · critical path 6.95433 ns · wirelength 197938 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 4.07248e+08 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 8.90%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 8.90%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

  reg-to-reg period (ns)    6.95433           6.52544           -6.17%   D/D0 0.938

On this design, placement seeds alone move the unmodified design's area × delay by about 8.9%; a change of it inside that is not evidence of improvement.

Area is VPR's "Total used logic block area" in minimum-width transistor areas. On this architecture one DSP tile costs 253779 and one CLB 27905; mac_18x19_fixed_pt_mode packs two blocks to a tile, so a block that does not shorten the slowest stage is paid for at about 4.5 CLBs when it shares a tile and about 9 when it does not (the blocks of a tile share one reset and one clock).
